## Q59

Biology · both · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

> ⚠ The independent solve chose (1); the booklet prints (2).

A colour blind man marries a woman with normal sight who has no history of colour blindness in her family. What is the probability of their grandson being colour blind?

- (1) 0.25
- **(2)** 0.5
- (3) 1
- (4) Nil

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (1) · differs

**Publisher's solution**

1. Father (Colourblind)
2. $\downarrow$ Daughter (Carrier)
3. $\downarrow$ Grandson [$50\%$ Probability ($0.5$)]

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Colour blindness is X-linked recessive: man is $X^cY$, woman with no family history is $XX$.
2. Their children: daughters $X^cX$ (carriers), sons $XY$ (normal).
3. A grandson through a carrier daughter (married to a normal man $XY$) is colour blind with probability $\frac{1}{2}$.
4. A grandson through a normal son receives no $X^c$, so probability $0$.
5. Taking either line equally likely: $\frac{1}{2}\times\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2}\times 0 = 0.25$, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Colour Blindness · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 19)

![printed question 59](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q059_3345be0b_28102026.png?v=3345be0bb536)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 59](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q059_de1152de_28102026.png?v=de1152de5dc8)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Calculation** (`calculate`) — put numbers in and get a number out
- **Concept:** X-linked recessive inheritance over two generations: an affected father gives his single X to every daughter, so each carrier daughter passes colour blindness to half her sons
- **Trap:** **Answers another question** (`answers_other_question`) — true, and about this topic, but it answers something else — option 4  
  Working the cross one generation gives a clean set of children, with no colour blind son and no colour blind daughter, and Nil is the right answer to that cross. The stem asks about the grandson, and every daughter is a carrier whose sons are affected half the time, which gives $0.5$.
- **Why:** This question requires a probability computed from a two-generation X-linked cross, demonstrated by the stem supplying both parents' phenotypes and the absence of family history and then asking for the probability of their grandson being colour blind.
- **Note:** Tagged as printed. The key $0.5$ follows the line through the carrier daughter; the stem does not say whether the grandson descends from the son or the daughter, and on the unconditional reading the figure is $0.25$, which is option 1, so the verdict records an arguable key rather than re-judging it.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q60

Biology · zoology · Human Reproduction

Ectopic pregnancies are referred to as :

- (1) Pregnancies terminated due to hormonal imbalance
- (2) Pregnancies with genetic abnormality.
- **(3)** Implantation of embryo at site other than uterus.
- (4) Implantation of defective embryo in the uterus

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Any extra uterine pregnancy is ectopic pregnancy.
2. Implantation can occur in the wall of abdominal cavity, ovaries but $90-95\%$ of ectopic pregnancies are tubal pregnancy where implantation occurs in fallopian tube.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Ectopic pregnancy means the embryo implants at a site other than the uterus (for example the fallopian tube), which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Implantation and Embryonic Development · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 19)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 60](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q060_9e523098_28102026.png?v=9e523098cc50)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Ectopic pregnancy is defined by the place of implantation: the embryo settles anywhere other than the uterine wall
- **Trap:** **Wrong site attacked** (`wrong_site_attacked`) — the reagent is right but it attacks the other site, atom or bond than the one the question turns on — option 4  
  This option opens with the key's own word, implantation, so a student who recalls only that ectopic pregnancy is an implantation that has gone wrong keeps the site they were taught, the uterus, and puts the fault into the embryo instead. The term is positional: what is out of place is the site, not the embryo.

## Q64

Biology · both · Biomolecules

Which of the following biomolecules does have a phosphodiester bond?

- **(1)** Nucleic acids in a nucleotide
- (2) Fatty acids in a diglyceride
- (3) Monosaccharides in a polysaccharide
- (4) Amino acids in a polypeptide

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Phosphodiester bond is formed between two nucleotides of nucleic acid.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In a nucleic acid, the $3'$-OH of one nucleotide's sugar and the $5'$-OH of the next are linked through a phosphate, forming a $3' \to 5'$ phosphodiester bond.
2. Fatty acids in a glyceride are linked to glycerol by ester bonds, monosaccharides in a polysaccharide by glycosidic bonds, and amino acids in a polypeptide by peptide bonds, so options $2$, $3$, $4$ are out.
3. Only option $1$ involves phosphodiester bonds.

**Subtopic:** Nucleic Acid · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 20)

![printed question 64](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q064_166c9554_28102026.png?v=166c9554f6a9)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 64](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q064_25c88533_28102026.png?v=25c88533d014)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Each class of biomolecule has its own linkage: phosphodiester between nucleotides, ester in fats, glycosidic between sugars, peptide between amino acids
- **Trap:** **look alike term** (`look_alike_term`) — two terms RESEMBLE each other and the resemblance is the pull: maturation / meristematic, lyase / ligase, GPP / NPP — option 2  
  The linkage in a diglyceride is called the ester bond and the word asked for carries 'ester' inside it, so a student keying bond names by the word lands on fats. A diglyceride holds no phosphate, and its ester joins glycerol to a fatty acid, not two halves of a phosphate.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated linkage that belongs to each class of biomolecule, demonstrated by 'does have a phosphodiester bond' being asked of four options that each merely name a monomer-in-polymer pair, with no criterion printed from which the bond could be worked out. Swap test applied: replace the glyceride's ester with a bond name unlike 'phosphodiester' and the pull dies, so the likeness of the names is doing the work.
- **Note:** The E1 pattern says statement_options, but the four options are noun phrases of the form 'X in Y', not statements to be judged, so the shape recorded is mcq. The keyed option reads 'Nucleic acids in a nucleotide', which inverts the containment the other three options use; the linkage meant is nucleotides inside a nucleic acid. Tagged as printed, not re-judged.

## Q65

Biology · zoology · Digestion and Absorption

The primary dentition in human differs from permanent dentition in not having one of the following type of teeth:

- (1) Incisors
- (2) Canine
- **(3)** Premolars
- (4) Molars

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Dental formula of human adult (permanent dentition) $= \frac{2123}{2123}$.
2. Dental formula of child (primary dentition) $= \frac{2102}{2102}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Primary (milk) dentition formula per half jaw: $\frac{2102}{2102}$, i.e. $I=2, C=1, PM=0, M=2$, total $2\times(5+5)=20$ teeth.
2. Permanent dentition formula per half jaw: $\frac{2123}{2123}$, i.e. $I=2, C=1, PM=2, M=3$, total $32$ teeth.
3. Comparing, $PM=0$ in primary dentition, so premolars are the type absent, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Human Digestive System: Alimentary Canal and Associated Glands · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 20)

- **Concept:** The deciduous (milk) set reads 2102 in each half jaw, so premolars arrive only with the permanent teeth
- **Trap:** **look alike term** (`look_alike_term`) — two terms RESEMBLE each other and the resemblance is the pull: maturation / meristematic, lyase / ligase, GPP / NPP — option 4

- **Why:** This question requires the stated composition of the deciduous dental set, demonstrated by 'differs from permanent dentition in not having one of the following type of teeth' being answered by naming one of four tooth types with nothing else printed to reason from. ## Q69

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

Male gametophyte in angiosperms produces:

- (3) Single sperm and a vegetative cell
- (4) Single sperm and two vegetative cells

2. Polyp reproduces asexually by budding to form medusa and medusa reproduces sexually to form polyp.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Metagenesis is the alternation of an asexual polyp phase with a sexual medusa phase in cnidarians such as $Obelia$, which is option $3$.

## Q74

Biology · botany · Environmental Issues

Eutrophication of water bodies leading to killing of fishes is mainly due to non-availability of :

- **(1)** Oxygen

- (3) Light

**Publisher's solution**

## Q76

Biology · botany · Microbes in Human Welfare

Match the following list of microbes and their importance:

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Sacharomyces cerevisiae | (i) | Production of immunosuppressive agents |
| (b) | Monascus purpureus | (ii) | Ripening of Swiss cheese |
| (c) | Trichoderma polysporum | (iii) | Commercial production of ethanol |
| (d) | Propionibacterium shermanii | (iv) | Production of blood-cholesterol lowering agents |

- (1) (a)-(iii), (b)-(i), (c)-(iv), (d)-(ii)
- (2) (a)-(iii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(ii)
- (3) (a)-(iv), (b)-(iii), (c)-(ii), (d)-(i)
- (4) (a)-(iv), (b)-(ii), (c)-(i), (d)-(iii)

**Answer** — booklet: (none) · independent solve: (2) · unchecked

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $\textit{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}$ (brewer's yeast) is used for commercial ethanol production, so (a)-(iii).
2. $\textit{Monascus purpureus}$ yields statins, blood-cholesterol lowering agents, so (b)-(iv).
3. $\textit{Trichoderma polysporum}$ yields cyclosporin A, an immunosuppressive agent, so (c)-(i).
4. $\textit{Propionibacterium shermanii}$ produces CO$_2$ that forms the holes in Swiss cheese during ripening, so (d)-(ii).
5. The match (a)-(iii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(ii) is option $2$.

  The two drug rows, (i) immunosuppressive agents and (iv) blood-cholesterol lowering agents, belong to one subtopic and are carried together as 'the two fungi that give drugs', so a student holding the pair without the names attached assigns them in printed order and reads (b)-(i), (c)-(iv).
- **Why:** This question requires the microbe-to-product pairings the chapter states, demonstrated by '(c) Trichoderma polysporum' being settled only by the stored fact that it yields '(i) Production of immunosuppressive agents', with no criterion printed from which the pairing could be worked out.

## Q77

Biology · both · Cell Cycle and Cell Division

Arrange the following events of meiosis in correct sequence

- **((a))** Crossing over
- **((b))** Synapsis
- **((c))** Terminalisation of chaismata
- **((d))** Disappearance of nucleolus

- (1) (b), (c), (d), (a)
- (2) (b), (a), (d), (c)
- **(3)** (b), (a), (c), (d)
- (4) (a), (b), (c), (d)

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The sequence of event during meiosis are
2. (1) Synapsis (Zygotene)
3. (2) Crossing over (Pachytene)
4. (3) Terminalisation of chiasmata
5. (4) Disappearance of nucleolus

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Synapsis, the pairing of homologous chromosomes, takes place in zygotene, so $(b)$ comes first.
2. Crossing over takes place between paired homologues in pachytene, so $(a)$ comes second.
3. Chiasmata terminalise during diakinesis, so $(c)$ comes third.
4. The nucleolus disappears at the end of diakinesis, just before metaphase I, so $(d)$ comes last.
5. The order $(b) \rightarrow (a) \rightarrow (c) \rightarrow (d)$ is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Prophase I and Its Types · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 23)

![printed question 77](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q077_88328986_28102026.png?v=88328986e2f2)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 77](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q077_88e11648_28102026.png?v=88e11648e164)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Sequence** (`order`) — a sequence to put in the right order, or a trend to arrange
- **Concept:** Prophase I runs zygotene to pachytene to diplotene to diakinesis, so homologues pair before they exchange segments, the chiasmata terminalise next, and the nucleolus is gone only in the last sub-stage
- **Trap:** **Wrong site attacked** (`wrong_site_attacked`) — the reagent is right but it attacks the other site, atom or bond than the one the question turns on — option 2  
  In mitotic prophase the nucleolus goes as soon as the chromosomes condense, and a student carrying that picture into prophase I slots the nucleolus in before the chiasmata terminalise, giving (b), (a), (d), (c).
- **Why:** This question requires the four prophase I events to be put in their taught sub-stage sequence, demonstrated by the stem 'Arrange the following events of meiosis in correct sequence' and by four options that differ only in the order of the same lettered items (a) to (d).
- **Note:** E1's pattern returned 'arrange_in_order', a retired value with no counterpart on the current qtype axis; the printed shape is a lettered list (a)-(d) above the options, which rule 1 makes 'statements', and the ordering demand is carried by asks: order rather than by qtype. Item (c) is printed 'Terminalisation of chaismata'; read as chiasmata and tagged as printed. Key (3) checks out against the sub-stage order zygotene, pachytene, diplotene, diakinesis.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q83

- (1) $\mathrm{O_3}$ and dust

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

## Q86

Biology · zoology · Locomotion and Movement

Which of the following is not a function of the skeletal system?

- (1) Locomotion
- (2) Production of erythrocytes
- (3) Storage of minerals
- **(4)** Production of body heat

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Production of body heat is the function of adipose tissue.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The skeletal system provides support and acts as levers for $\text{locomotion}$ (option $1$) together with muscles.
2. Red bone marrow carries out haemopoiesis, producing erythrocytes (option $2$).
3. Bone matrix stores minerals such as $\text{Ca}^{2+}$ and phosphate (option $3$).
4. Body heat is produced mainly by the metabolic activity and contraction of skeletal muscles, not by bones, so option $4$ is not a skeletal function.

## Q89

Biology · botany · Biological Classification

Select the wrong statement:

- (1) Mosaic disease in tobacco and AIDS in human being are caused by viruses
- **(2)** The viroids were discovered by D.J. Ivanowski
- (3) W.M. Stanley showed that viruses could be crystallized
- (4) The term 'contagium vivum fluidum' was coined by M.W. Beijerinek

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The viroids were discovered by T.O. Diener.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Tobacco mosaic disease (TMV) and AIDS (HIV) are both viral diseases, so option $1$ is correct.
2. Viroids were discovered by T.O. Diener in $1971$; Ivanowski ($1892$) only showed the tobacco mosaic agent passes bacteria-proof filters, so option $2$ is wrong.
3. W.M. Stanley ($1935$) showed viruses could be crystallised, so option $3$ is correct.
4. M.W. Beijerinck ($1898$) called the infectious fluid 'contagium vivum fluidum', so option $4$ is correct.
5. The wrong statement is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Viroids, Prions & Lichens · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 26)

- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 4  
  Ivanowski and Beijerinck are credited in consecutive sentences of the same account, so a student who holds both names but not their order reads 'contagium vivum fluidum' as Ivanowski's phrase and marks the Beijerinck statement as the wrong one.

- **Note:** Applied the 5.1 swap test to the two names: Ivanowski and Beijerinck do not resemble each other, and the pull survives their replacement by unlike words because the paragraph prints them in sequence, so nearby_fact. ## Q91

Biology · zoology · Excretory Products and Their Elimination

Human urine is usually acidic because:

- **(1)** Hydrogen ions are actively secreted into the filtrate.
- (2) The sodium transporter exchanges one hydrogen ion for each sodium ion, in peritubular capillaries.
- (3) Excreted plasma proteins are acidic
- (4) Potassium and sodium exchange generates acidity

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Tubular secretion maintains the pH and ionic balance of body fluids in which hydrogen ions are actively secreted into the filterate and bicarbonate ions are reabsorbed.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The PCT and DCT actively secrete $\mathrm{H^+}$ into the filtrate (tubular secretion) to keep the body's acid-base balance; this added $\mathrm{H^+}$ makes urine slightly acidic ($\mathrm{pH} \approx 6$), which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Function of the Tubules and their types · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 26)

- **Concept:** Urine is acidified because the tubule actively secretes H+ into the filtrate
- **Trap:** **Wrong site attacked** (`wrong_site_attacked`) — the reagent is right but it attacks the other site, atom or bond than the one the question turns on — option 2  
  The sodium-for-hydrogen exchange named in the option is a real tubular transport step, so a student who remembers that an antiporter trades Na+ in for H+ out accepts the sentence whole. That exchange works across the tubular wall into the filtrate, not 'in peritubular capillaries', where no urine is being formed.
- **Why:** This question requires the cause the textbook gives for urine's acidity, demonstrated by the stem 'Human urine is usually acidic because:' and four options each offering a rival mechanism.
- **Note:** E1 recorded statement_options, a retired value; the stem is a trailing 'because' with full-sentence options, which under brief rule 1 is still mcq. half_right was available because option 2 has a mechanism part and a location part, but wrong_site_attacked names which part fails and so was preferred.

## Q93

Biology · zoology · Human Reproduction

Which of the following events is not associated with ovulation in human female?

- (1) LH surge
- **(2)** Decrease in estradiol
- (3) Full development of Graafian follicle
- (4) Release of secondary oocyte

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In $28$ days reproductive cycle, ovulation occurs on $14$th day due to LH surge.
2. In the mid cycle, the level of FSH and estrogen are also high.
3. The female gamete is released from the ovary in secondary oocyte stage after completing meiosis I.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. At mid-cycle the fully developed Graafian follicle secretes rising estrogen (estradiol), which triggers the LH surge; the LH surge causes rupture of the Graafian follicle and release of the secondary oocyte, so options $1$, $3$ and $4$ are associated with ovulation while a decrease in estradiol is not, giving option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Menstrual Cycle · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 27)

![printed question 93](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q093_fb87ab81_28102026.png?v=fb87ab812842)

---

<!--ecat-->

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** At mid-cycle the estrogen peak drives the LH surge, which ruptures the mature Graafian follicle and releases the secondary oocyte
- **Trap:** **Answers another question** (`answers_other_question`) — true, and about this topic, but it answers something else — option 1  
  The LH surge is the event printed as inducing ovulation, so it is the first thing retrieved when the stem's topic is ovulation, and reading the stem as 'which event is associated' puts it first. It is true of ovulation, which is exactly why it cannot answer a stem asking for the event not associated.
- **Why:** This question requires which printed events accompany ovulation, demonstrated by the stem asking for the event 'not associated with ovulation' while three options name events the cycle's account places at mid-cycle.

## Q94

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

Body having meshwork of cells, internal cavities lined with food filtering flagellated cells and indirect development are the characteristics of phylum:

- (1) Protozoa
- (2) Coelenterata
- **(3)** Porifera
- (4) Mollusca

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In poriferans, the body is loose aggregate of cells (meshwork of cells).
2. Internal cavities and canals are lined with food filtering flagellated cells i.e. choanocyte/collar cell.
3. Choanocytes help in filter feeding.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Cellular-level body with a water canal system and spongocoel lined by food-filtering flagellated choanocytes (collar cells), with indirect development through a larva, defines phylum Porifera, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Phylum – Porifera · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q95

Biology · zoology · Chemical Coordination and Integration

Which one of the following hormones is not involved in sugar metabolism?

- (1) Glucagon
- (2) Cortisone
- **(3)** Aldosterone
- (4) Insulin

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Mineralocorticoid (Aldosterone) has no role in sugar metabolism.
2. It helps in salt metabolism.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Glucagon raises and insulin lowers blood glucose, and cortisone (a glucocorticoid) promotes gluconeogenesis, but aldosterone (a mineralocorticoid) controls only $Na^+$, $K^+$ and water balance, so the answer is option $3$.

The same printed paragraph also gives glucocorticoids gluconeogenesis, so the hormone outside sugar metabolism is the mineralocorticoid aldosterone.

## Q96

Biology · zoology · Human Health and Disease

Which of the following diseases is caused by a protozoan?

- (1) Blastomycosis
- (2) Syphilis
- (3) Influenza
- **(4)** Babesiosis

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Babesiosis is a disease caused by a protozoan, Babesia bigemina.
2. The vector is tick, so disease is also called tick fever in cattle.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Blastomycosis is a fungal disease (Blastomyces), syphilis is bacterial (Treponema pallidum) and influenza is viral, while babesiosis is caused by the protozoan Babesia, so the answer is option $4$.

## Q97

Biology · both · Strategies for Enhancement in Food Production

Outbreeding is an important strategy of animal husbandry because it :

- (1) Exposes harmful recessive genes that are eliminated by selection
- (2) Helps in accumulation of superior genes.
- (3) Is useful in producing purelines of animals.
- **(4)** Is useful in overcoming inbreeding depression

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. A single outcross (a type of outbreeding) is useful in overcoming inbreeding depression.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Exposing harmful recessives, accumulating superior genes and producing purelines are all advantages of inbreeding as NCERT teaches it; outbreeding, i.e. mating unrelated animals, is used to overcome inbreeding depression, which is option $4$.

## Q99

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

A jawless fish, which lays eggs in fresh water and whose ammocoetes larvae after metamorphosis return to the ocean is:

- **(1)** Petromyzon
- (2) Eptatretus
- (3) Myxine
- (4) Neomyxine

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Petromyzon (Lamprey) is a migratory marine water jawless fish which shows anadromous migration.
2. It spawns in fresh water, stops feeding and dies.
3. Its larva (Ammocoetes) after metamorphosis will return to ocean.

NCERT: cyclostomes such as $Petromyzon$ (lamprey) are marine but migrate to fresh water for spawning, and after a few days their larvae metamorphose and return to the ocean; the ammocoete larva belongs to lampreys, whereas $Myxine$, $Eptatretus$ and $Neomyxine$ are marine hagfishes without a larval stage, so the answer is option $1$.

## Q101

Biology · zoology · Body Fluids and Circulation

If you suspect major deficiency of antibodies in a person, to which of the following would you look for confirmatory evidence?

- **(1)** Serum globulins
- (2) Fibrinogin in plasma
- (3) Serum albumins
- (4) Haemocytes

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

1. Antibodies are present in serum.

## Q102

Biology · zoology · Human Reproduction

In human females, meiosis-II is not completed until?

- (1) Birth
- (2) Puberty
- **(3)** Fertilization
- (4) Uterine implantation

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In human females, meiosis II is completed after the entry of sperm into the cytoplasm of secondary oocyte at the time of fertilisation leading to the formation of ovum and IInd polar body.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The secondary oocyte released at ovulation stays arrested in meiosis-II; sperm entry at fertilization induces completion of meiosis-II, giving the ovum (ootid) and the second polar body, so the answer is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Female Gametogenesis (Oogenesis) · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 28)

<!--ecat-->

- **Concept:** Meiosis in the human oocyte stops twice: meiosis-I finishes at ovulation, and meiosis-II only when a sperm enters the secondary oocyte

  Puberty is the stage the oogenesis account attaches the restart of meiosis to, since the primary oocyte lies arrested until then, and a student carries that same stage over to the completion of meiosis-II, which happens only at fertilisation.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated timing of the second meiotic division in the human oocyte, demonstrated by the stem asking when meiosis-II 'is not completed until' while all four options are life-history stages rather than cell events. - **Concept:** Parthenocarpy is fruit set without fertilisation, so the fruit is seedless, and banana is the named example
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 3  
  Apple is the one fruit this chapter singles out, but it is singled out as a false fruit, and that sentence sits beside the parthenocarpy line, so a student holding 'apple is the special fruit here' brings it to the wrong question.
- **Why:** This question requires one named example of a stated condition, demonstrated by 'Which one of the following fruits is parthenocarpic?' with 'Apple', the same paragraph's false-fruit example, standing among the options. Apple is tagged nearby_fact on the swap test: apple and banana resemble each other in no way, so position in the paragraph is what carries the student, and the pull survives any renaming.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q106

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

In angiosperms, microsporogenesis and megasporogenesis :

- (1) Occur in ovule
- (2) Occur in anther
- (3) Form gametes without furthers divisions
- **(4)** Involve meiosis

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In angiosperms, microsporogenesis and megasporogenesis involve meiosis

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Microsporogenesis occurs in the anther, megasporogenesis in the ovule, so options $1$ and $2$ each describe only one process.
2. Spores formed undergo further mitotic divisions to form the gametophytes that produce gametes, so option $3$ is false.
3. Both processes form haploid ($n$) spores from diploid ($2n$) mother cells by meiosis, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Microsporangium and Microsporogenesis, Megasporogenesis · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 29)

![printed question 106](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q106_ad41b3cf_28102026.png?v=ad41b3cfb5de)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 106](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q106_8116dec2_28102026.png?v=8116dec2555f)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Comparison** (`compare`) — the difference between two things, or the correct pairing
- **Concept:** Sporogenesis is the meiotic step in both sexes: microspores are formed in the anther and megaspores in the ovule, but the division itself is meiosis in each case
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 2  
  Microsporogenesis is named first in the stem and the anther is the site drilled hardest for it, so a student answers for that half of the stem and stops; the option is false of megasporogenesis, which runs inside the ovule.
- **Why:** This question requires holding two neighbouring processes apart before a shared property can be chosen, demonstrated by 'Occur in ovule' and 'Occur in anther' each being true of exactly one of the two processes the stem names together, so only 'Involve meiosis' survives for both.
- **Note:** Option 3 is copied as printed, including the typographical slip in 'Form gametes without furthers divisions'. This is the one member of the group whose stem names two processes at once, which is why its asks differs from the group.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q107

Biology · both · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

A gene showing codominance has:

- **(1)** Both alleles independently expressed in the heterozygote
- (2) One allele dominant on the other
- (3) Alleles tightly linked on the same chromosome
- (4) Alleles that are recessive to each other

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Both alleles are independently expressed in heterozygote during codominance.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In codominance the $F_1$ heterozygote expresses both alleles fully and independently (e.g. $I^A I^B$ gives blood group AB), which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Incomplete Dominance & Co-dominance · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 29)

![printed question 107](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q107_251f59bf_28102026.png?v=251f59bff8dd)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 107](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q107_d43d49ce_28102026.png?v=d43d49ce2e21)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Codominance: both alleles of a gene are expressed in the heterozygote, so neither one masks the other
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 4  
  Codominance is carried in memory as 'neither allele is dominant over the other', and a student holding only that negative restates it as each allele being recessive to the other, which inverts the relation: both alleles are expressed, not both masked.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated definition of one genetic term, demonstrated by 'Both alleles independently expressed in the heterozygote' set against 'One allele dominant on the other'. Option 4 is tagged reversed rather than look_alike_term because it keeps the symmetry of the definition and inverts its direction, turning both expressed into both masked.
- **Note:** The pattern classifier called this statement_options; the printed shape is a stem completion, 'A gene showing codominance has:', whose options are predicates finishing the sentence rather than standalone statements put up for judgement, so it is recorded as mcq with the rest of the group.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q108

Biology · both · Biomolecules

The chitinous exoskeleton of arthropods is formed by the polymerisation of :

- (1) Lipoglycans
- (2) Keratin sulphate and chondroitin sulphate
- (3) D-glucosamine

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Exoskeleton of arthropods is made up of chitin.
2. Chitin is a polymer of N-acetyl glucosamine.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 29)

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Chitin is a polymer of the amino sugar N-acetyl glucosamine, not of plain glucosamine
- **Trap:** **look alike term** (`look_alike_term`) — two terms RESEMBLE each other and the resemblance is the pull: maturation / meristematic, lyase / ligase, GPP / NPP — option 3  
  Chitin is carried as 'a polymer of an amino sugar', and D-glucosamine is the amino sugar whose name sits inside the right answer, so the shorter name reads as the monomer. The acetyl group belongs to the monomer; the deacetylated polymer is chitosan, not chitin.

- **Concept:** Deuteromycetes are the imperfect fungi, known only in asexual or vegetative stages, and many are litter decomposers in mineral cycling

  The line printed beside the imperfect fungi says that once their sexual forms were discovered they were moved into the class they rightly belong to, and Ascomycetes is that destination, so the reclassified home is read as the answer to 'belong to'. The stem asks for the class these fungi sit in while only asexual stages are known, which is Deuteromycetes.

## Q110

Biology · both · Evolution

The wings of a bird and the wings of an insect are :

- (1) Homologous structures and represent convergent evolution
- (2) Homologous structures and represent divergent evolution
- **(3)** Analogous structures and represent convergent evolution
- (4) Phylogenetic structures and represent divergent evolution

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The wings of a bird and an insect are analogous structure which differ in structure and origin but perform similar functions and represent convergent evolution.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Bird and insect wings both work for flight but are built differently and come from different origins, so they are analogous structures that show convergent evolution (option $3$).

**Subtopic:** Homology and Analogy · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 30)

![printed question 110](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q110_42c472d6_28102026.png?v=42c472d6b009)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 110](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q110_5549e3c2_28102026.png?v=5549e3c26be8)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Two answers** (`two_part`) — the answer has two halves, usually joined by 'respectively'
- **Asks:** **Comparison** (`compare`) — the difference between two things, or the correct pairing
- **Concept:** Same function from different ancestry is analogy, produced by convergent evolution; shared ancestry put to different uses is homology, produced by divergence
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 1  
  Two wings doing the same job with no shared ancestry is correctly read as convergent evolution, and 'homologous' is then carried over as the word for any structures that match in function, which this option keeps beside the right second half. Homology needs shared ancestry and structure -- a bird's wing is a bony forelimb, an insect's a fold of cuticle -- so only the evolution half of the option holds.
- **Why:** This question requires holding homology against analogy and divergence against convergence, demonstrated by the options being crossings of those two pairs -- 'Homologous structures and represent convergent evolution' against 'Analogous structures and represent convergent evolution' -- so each half has to be decided separately.
- **Note:** E1 pattern returned the retired value statement_options on 15 full-statement options. The printed shape is four options each coupling a structure verdict with an evolution verdict, accepted or rejected whole, so I recorded qtype two_part; there is no printed block of statements to judge, which rules out statements.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q111

Biology · botany · Morphology of Flowering Plants

Flower are unisexual in:

- (1) Onion
- (2) Pea

- (4) China rose

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Flowers are unisexual in cucumber.
2. [Family - Cucurbitaceae]

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Onion, pea and China rose all bear bisexual flowers, while cucumber is monoecious with separate staminate and pistillate (unisexual) flowers, so the answer is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** The Flower · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 30)

![printed question 111](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q111_a7aa5051_28102026.png?v=a7aa50511f79)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 111](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q111_ee60d0e4_28102026.png?v=ee60d0e468a1)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** A unisexual flower bears either stamens or carpels, and the cucurbits are the taught example of it
- **Trap:** **No trap** (`no_trap`) — nothing pulls; the other options are only filler  
  Checked each of the other three against the flower description that covers it: the Fabaceae account covering pea prints the flower as bisexual and zygomorphic, the Liliaceae account covering onion prints it as bisexual and actinomorphic, and china rose is the standard hypogynous example drawn with both stamens and stigma, so no printed line offers unisexuality to any of the three, and the stem prints no criterion to apply instead.
- **Why:** This question requires the stated fact of which named plant bears unisexual flowers, demonstrated by the stem 'Flower are unisexual in:' followed by four coordinate crop plants with no criterion printed to apply.
- **Note:** Three fillers, so I looked again before recording no_trap: the discrimination here rests entirely on knowing the cucurbits are monoecious, and all three wrong plants are printed as bisexual in their own family or flower descriptions, which leaves no route to walk. The stem reads 'Flower are unisexual in:' in our record, which is a grammatical slip for 'Flowers'; it does not change what is asked, and I tagged the question as printed.

## Q112

Biology · botany · Environmental Issues

Increase in concentration of the toxicant at successive trophic levels is known as :

- (1) Biogeochemical cycling
- **(2)** Biomagnification
- (3) Biodeterioration
- (4) Biotransformation

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Increase in concentration of toxic substances in successive trophic level.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. A toxicant that accumulates in organisms and cannot be metabolised or excreted reaches higher concentrations at each successive trophic level; this is called biomagnification, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Water Pollution and Its Control · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 30)

![printed question 112](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q112_4e13a396_28102026.png?v=4e13a396b85e)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 112](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q112_8d021246_28102026.png?v=8d021246b1a0)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Biomagnification: a non-degradable toxicant rises in concentration as it is passed up the trophic levels
- **Trap:** **Neighbouring fact** (`nearby_fact`) — a true fact about a neighbouring thing is offered instead — option 1  
  'Successive trophic levels' is the phrase the ecosystem material attaches to the movement of matter along the food chain, so a student holding that association answers with the cycling term for a stem that is actually about concentration rising. Biogeochemical cycling is matter returning between the biotic and abiotic pools, which says nothing about a toxicant becoming more concentrated at each level.
- **Why:** This question requires the printed term for a stated phenomenon, demonstrated by the stem 'Increase in concentration of the toxicant at successive trophic levels is known as :' with four coordinate 'bio-' terms as options.
- **Note:** Applied the swap test on the chosen label: replace 'Biogeochemical cycling' with an unlike phrase of the same meaning, such as 'nutrient cycling', and the pull survives, because it is the stem's own words 'successive trophic levels' doing the work, not any resemblance to the word biomagnification. So nearby_fact, not look_alike_term.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q113

Biology · zoology · Neural Control and Coordination

Destruction of the anterior horn cells of the spinal cord would result in loss of :-

- (1) Integrating impulses
- (2) Sensory impulses
- **(3)** Voluntary motor impulses
- (4) Commissural impulses

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Anterior horn cells are ventral horn cells of spinal cord which consists of motor neurons.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The anterior (ventral) horn of the spinal cord grey matter contains the cell bodies of motor neurons whose axons leave via the ventral root to skeletal muscles; their destruction abolishes voluntary motor impulses, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Human Neural System and Its Divisions · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 30)

![printed question 113](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q113_035b6450_28102026.png?v=035b64508fdb)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 113](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q113_d290c779_28102026.png?v=d290c779e9ea)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Rule applied** (`apply_rule`) — a rule you already know, used on a case you have not seen
- **Concept:** The anterior (ventral) horn of the spinal cord holds the cell bodies of the somatic motor neurons; the posterior horn is where sensory fibres come in
- **Trap:** **Reversed** (`reversed`) — the right thing stated backwards: direction, order or ratio — option 2  
  A student who holds the pair 'one horn is sensory, the other is motor' without which horn is which attaches the sensory half to the anterior horn, and the stem's 'would result in loss of' accepts either half equally. The sensory fibres enter at the posterior horn through the dorsal root; the anterior horn holds the motor cell bodies whose axons leave by the ventral root.
- **Why:** This question requires the dorsal-sensory, ventral-motor rule applied to a printed lesion, demonstrated by the stem 'Destruction of the anterior horn cells of the spinal cord would result in loss of :-' asking for the consequence rather than for the name of a part.
- **Note:** Ruled out wrong_site_attacked for the chosen trap: the lesion site is printed in the stem and the student does not move it, so what inverts is which horn is credited with which traffic, which is reversed.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q114

Biology · botany · Transport in Plants

Roots play insignificant role in absorption of water in :

- (1) Wheat
- (2) Sunflower
- **(3)** Pistia
- (4) Pea

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Pistia - roots are poorly developed as it is free floating hydrophyte.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $\text{Pistia}$ is a free-floating hydrophyte surrounded by water, so it absorbs water through its whole body surface and its roots play an insignificant role in absorption, while wheat, sunflower and pea are mesophytes that depend on roots; this is option $3$.

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** A free-floating aquatic plant takes in water over its whole body surface, so the root is not the absorbing organ it is in a land plant
- **Trap:** **No trap** (`no_trap`) — nothing pulls; the other options are only filler  
  Checked each remaining option against the stem: wheat, sunflower and pea are all land crops whose roots are the absorbing organ, the stem prints no root feature that would single one of them out, and the chapter states nothing beside the floating-aquatic fact that would carry a student to any one of the three; the whole question turns on recognising Pistia as the floater
- **Why:** This question requires the stated fact about which named plant does not depend on its roots for water, demonstrated by the stem printing no criterion at all and the four options being four plant names, 'Wheat', 'Sunflower', 'Pistia' and 'Pea'.

## Q115

Biology · both · Cell: The Unit of Life

Match the columns and identify the correct option:

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Thylakoids | (i) | Disc-Shaped sacs in Golgi apparatus |
| (b) | Cristae | (ii) | Condensed structure of DNA |
| (c) | Cisternae | (iii) | Flat membranous sacs in stroma |
| (d) | Chromatin | (iv) | Infoldings in mitochondria |

- (1) (iii) (iv) (ii) (i)
- (2) (iv) (iii) (i) (ii)
- (3) (iii) (iv) (i) (ii)
- (4) (iii) (i) (iv) (ii)

**Answer** — booklet: (none) · independent solve: (3) · unchecked

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Thylakoids $\to$ (iii) flat membranous sacs in stroma; cristae $\to$ (iv) infoldings in mitochondria; cisternae $\to$ (i) disc-shaped sacs in Golgi apparatus; chromatin $\to$ (ii) condensed structure of DNA, so the sequence (iii) (iv) (i) (ii) is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Cell Organelles - Comparative Overview · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 30)

![printed question 115](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q115_0f7611ef_28102026.png?v=0f7611efb7f7)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Match the columns** (`match_columns`) — two labelled lists to pair, the answer is a pairing code
- **Asks:** **Stated fact** (`fact_line`) — a fact NCERT states: what, who or where
- **Concept:** Each organelle's internal compartment has its own name and its own home: thylakoids in the chloroplast stroma, cristae in the mitochondrion, cisternae in the Golgi, chromatin as condensed DNA
- **Trap:** **look alike term** (`look_alike_term`) — two terms RESEMBLE each other and the resemblance is the pull: maturation / meristematic, lyase / ligase, GPP / NPP — option 4  
  Cristae and cisternae differ by two letters and are printed one under the other as (b) and (c), so a student who stores both as 'stacked membrane compartments' and keeps them apart by the word alone reads the pair the other way round and takes (b)-(i), (c)-(iv).
- **Why:** This question requires the stated description of each named organelle part, demonstrated by '(iv) Infoldings in mitochondria' naming its own organelle, so each row is settled by the printed definition rather than by sorting the four terms against one another.
- **Note:** The packet carries correct_answer: null and no answer line, so no key could be followed; the labels are derived from the printed lists, where (a)-(iii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(ii) is the only option consistent with the descriptions as printed. The options list only List II labels, in (a) to (d) order, without repeating the List I labels; read that way throughout.

## Q116

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

Identify the correct order of organisation of genetic material from largest to smallest :

- (1) Chromosome, genome, nucleotide, gene
- (2) Chromosome, gene, genome, nucleotide
- (3) Genome, chromosomes, nucleotide, gene
- **(4)** Genome, chromosome, gene, nucleotide

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Order of organisation of genetic material

![printed figure: solution_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q116_sol_1_e01e0ab1_28102026.png?v=e01e0ab145ea)


**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The genome is the whole genetic material, made up of all the chromosomes, so $\text{genome} > \text{chromosome}$; each chromosome carries many genes, so $\text{chromosome} > \text{gene}$; a gene is a DNA segment of many nucleotides, so $\text{gene} > \text{nucleotide}$. This gives option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Introduction to Molecular Basis of Inheritance · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 31)

![printed question 116](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q116_c70da499_28102026.png?v=c70da499c56e)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 116](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q116_a4e48fd8_28102026.png?v=a4e48fd87bc6)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Sequence** (`order`) — a sequence to put in the right order, or a trend to arrange
- **Concept:** The nesting of genetic material: a genome is the full set of chromosomes, a chromosome carries many genes, and a gene is a stretch of nucleotides
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 3  
  A student with the top of the hierarchy right reads 'nucleotide' as the long nucleotide chain, puts it above the gene, and so breaks only the last adjacency of an otherwise correct order
- **Why:** This question requires the taught nesting of genetic material to be put in size order, demonstrated by the stem asking for 'the correct order of organisation of genetic material from largest to smallest' with four options that differ only in sequence.
- **Note:** E1_question_type is 'arrange_in_order', a retired value; the printed shape is one question with four single answers, so qtype is mcq and the ordering is carried by asks.

## Q117

Biology · zoology · Chemical Coordination and Integration

Which one of the following hormones though synthesis elsewhere, is stored and released by the master gland?

- (1) Melanocyte stimulating hormone
- **(2)** Antidiuretic hormone
- (3) Luteinizing hormone
- (4) Prolactin

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Antidiuretic hormone is synthesized by the neurons of hypothalamus and stored in axon endings of posterior lobe of pituitary and released into the blood by posterior pituitary.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The posterior pituitary (part of the master gland) stores and releases oxytocin and $\text{ADH}$, which are synthesised in the hypothalamus; MSH, LH and prolactin are synthesised by the pituitary itself, so the answer is option $2$.

  'Synthesised elsewhere' sends a student hunting for the pituitary hormone with the odd source, and MSH is the one the chapter files under a different lobe, the pars intermedia, so the oddity printed beside the right fact is taken for it
- **Why:** This question requires the stated fact that the hormone stored in the posterior pituitary is made in the hypothalamus, demonstrated by the stem 'though synthesis elsewhere, is stored and released by the master gland'. Swap test applied for the trap: 'melanocyte stimulating hormone' and 'antidiuretic hormone' do not resemble each other, so position in the pituitary lobe list is doing the work, which is nearby_fact.

## Q118

Biology · botany · Anatomy of Flowering Plants

Read the different components from (a) to (d) in the list given below and tell the correct order of the components with reference to their arrangement from outer side to inner side in a woody dicot stem:
The correct order is :

- **((a))** Secondary cortex
- **((b))** Wood
- **((c))** Secondary phloem
- **((d))** Phellem

- (1) (d), (c), (a), (b)
- (2) (c), (d), (b), (a)
- (3) (a), (b), (d), (c)
- **(4)** (d), (a), (c), (b)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Sequence of different components of woody dicot stem from outerside to inner side is:

![printed figure: solution_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q118_sol_1_b4b10af6_28102026.png?v=b4b10af6f2c5)


**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Cork cambium (phellogen) cuts off phellem (cork) to the outside, so $(d)$ phellem is outermost.
2. Phellogen cuts off phelloderm (secondary cortex) to the inside, so $(a)$ secondary cortex comes next.
3. Vascular cambium cuts off secondary phloem to the outside, so $(c)$ secondary phloem lies inside the secondary cortex.
4. Vascular cambium cuts off secondary xylem (wood) to the inside, so $(b)$ wood is innermost.
5. Order $(d) \to (a) \to (c) \to (b)$ matches option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Secondary Growth · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 31)

![printed question 118](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q118_105b5608_28102026.png?v=105b56089312)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 118](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q118_03b55006_28102026.png?v=03b55006812a)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **Statements** (`statements`) — lettered statements, the answer is which combination is right
- **Asks:** **Sequence** (`order`) — a sequence to put in the right order, or a trend to arrange
- **Concept:** In a woody dicot stem each cambium lays its products on a fixed side: the cork cambium throws phellem outwards and secondary cortex inwards, the vascular cambium throws secondary phloem outwards and wood inwards
- **Trap:** **Partly correct** (`half_right`) — one part of the option is right and one part is wrong — option 1  
  Working outward from the vascular cambium gives phloem outside wood, and cork is known to be outermost, so secondary cortex is dropped into the one slot left over, behind the phloem; that yields (d), (c), (a), (b), which keeps both ends right and fails only on the middle adjacency.
- **Why:** This question requires the outer-to-inner sequence of the tissues the two cambia deposit in a woody dicot stem, demonstrated by the stem asking for 'the correct order of the components with reference to their arrangement from outer side to inner side' over a lettered block of four tissue names that the options only permute.
- **Note:** E1 read this as arrange_in_order, and the six-value qtype axis carries no ordering shape; the printed shape is a lettered block (a) to (d) above four options, so rule 1 ('prints a lettered list') puts qtype at statements while asks: order carries the sequencing, which is the information arrange_in_order held. The items are tissue names to sequence, not claims to judge, so a reviewer may want an ordered_list value on the qtype axis. Trap label chosen through the rulebook 5.0 route: the device is the retired sequence_endpoint_or_adjacency (ends right, one interior adjacency wrong), reversed excludes 'one swapped pair inside a longer order' by its own wording, so the live family is half_right on a multi-part option, two of whose four positions fail.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q119

Biology · zoology · Locomotion and Movement

## Q120

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

Which one of the following is not applicable to RNA?

- **(1)** Chargaff's rule
- (2) Complementary base pairing
- (3) 5' phosphoryl and 3' hydroxyl ends
- (4) Heterocyclic nitrogenous bases

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Chargaff's rule is applicable only for DNA.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Chargaff's rule ($A = T$, $G = C$, $\frac{A+G}{C+T} = 1$) follows from base pairing across two strands of double-stranded DNA; single-stranded RNA does not obey it, while RNA still has complementary base pairing (e.g. tRNA), $5'$ phosphoryl and $3'$ hydroxyl ends, and heterocyclic nitrogenous bases, so option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Salient Features of Structure of DNA · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 32)

![printed question 120](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q120_a31e287f_28102026.png?v=a31e287ffd17)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 120](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q120_8e1769a0_28102026.png?v=8e1769a0d0a8)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Comparison** (`compare`) — the difference between two things, or the correct pairing
- **Concept:** Chargaff's equivalence of A to T and G to C is a property of the two-stranded DNA molecule, not of nucleic acids in general
- **Trap:** **Condition dropped** (`condition_missed`) — drop the condition the stem states and the option becomes true — option 2  
  A student holding 'RNA is single-stranded' carries the pairing rule past the case it was learnt on and strikes out complementary base pairing, but RNA pairs with the DNA template as it is transcribed and with itself in the folded tRNA
- **Why:** This question requires DNA-only properties to be held apart from properties shared by both nucleic acids, demonstrated by the stem 'not applicable to RNA' with three of the four options true of RNA as well.
- **Note:** condition_missed rather than nearby_fact here: the pull on option 2 is a remembered rule, that pairing needs two strands, used past the point it holds, not the position of a name in a list.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q121

Biology · zoology · Body Fluids and Circulation

Doctors use stethoscope to hear the sound; produced during each cardiac cycle. The second sound is heard when:

- (2) AV valves open up

## Q122

Biology · botany · Ecosystem

During ecological succession:

- (1) The changes lead to a community that is in near equilibrium with the environment and is called pioneer community
- **(2)** The gradual and predictable change in species composition occurs in a given area
- (3) The establishment of a new biotic community is very fast in its primary phase
- (4) The number and types of animals remain constant

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Ecological succession is defined as the gradual and fairly predictable change in species composition of a given area, so option $2$ is correct; the near-equilibrium community is the climax community (option $1$ false), primary succession is very slow (option $3$ false), and the number and types of animals change (option $4$ false).

## Q123

Biology · both · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

In the following human pedigree, the filled symbols represent the affected individuals. Identify the type of given pedigree.

![printed figure: question](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q123_e652acd2_28102026.png?v=e652acd2a8c4)

- (1) X-linked dominant
- (2) Autosomal dominant
- (3) X-linked recessive
- **(4)** Autosomal recessive

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The given pedigree represents inheritance of Autosomal recessive trait.

![printed figure: solution_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q123_sol_1_baac6b50_28102026.png?v=baac6b50e20b)


**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In generation II, an unaffected man and an unaffected woman have an affected son in III, and in generation III two unaffected parents have two affected sons in IV, so the trait is recessive (dominant traits cannot skip from unaffected parents).
2. The affected woman in II ($X^aX^a$ if X-linked recessive) would pass $X^a$ to every son, so all her sons would be affected; her son in III is unaffected, so the trait is not X-linked recessive.
3. All matings fit autosomal recessive: affected $aa$ father I x carrier $Aa$ mother gives $aa$ and $Aa$ children; carrier $Aa \times Aa$ couples give $aa$ affected sons, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Pedigree Analysis · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 32)

![printed question 123](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q123_bd9ebbc8_28102026.png?v=bd9ebbc80bed)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 123](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q123_5de57708_28102026.png?v=5de5770869c2)

---

<!--ecat-->
#### Categories

- **Type:** **MCQ** (`mcq`) — a question and four answers, nothing lettered or listed
- **Asks:** **Figure reading** (`read_diagram`) — read a figure, graph or drawn structure and answer from it
- **Concept:** In a pedigree, affected children born to unaffected parents fix the trait as recessive, and the sexes of the affected individuals decide autosomal against X-linked
- **Trap:** **No trap** (`no_trap`) — nothing pulls; the other options are only filler  
  the answer is decided by the printed pedigree drawing and the figure was not supplied to this step, so which option the drawing pulls toward cannot be checked
- **Why:** This question requires reading the printed pedigree for the pattern of affected individuals, demonstrated by the stem's 'In the following human pedigree, the filled symbols represent the affected individuals.'
- **Note:** The stem names a figure and the four options are modes of inheritance, so the drawing alone decides between them; the image at figures_agent/2015_A/q123.png was not available to this step. The paper is intact, so this is not source damage. A reviewer holding the figure should check option 3, X-linked recessive, as the rival reading of any recessive pedigree: it is ruled out when an affected daughter has an unaffected father, or when affected individuals of both sexes appear in similar numbers. The same reviewer should check that an affected child with two unaffected parents is present, which is what excludes options 1 and 2.
<!--/ecat-->

## Q124

Biology · both · Cell: The Unit of Life

Balbiani rings are sites of :

- **(1)** RNA and protein synthesis
- (2) Lipid synthesis
- (3) Nucleotide synthesis
- (4) Polysaccharide synthesis

## Q127

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

Satellite DNA is important because it :

- (1) Codes for enzymes needed for DNA replication
- (2) Codes for proteins needed in cell cycle
- **(3)** Shows high degree of polymorphism in population and also the same degree of polymorphism in an individual, which is heritable from parents to children
- (4) Does not code for proteins and is same in all members of the population

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Satellite DNA are the repetitive DNA which do not code for any protein.
2. They show high degree of polymorphism and form basis of DNA fingerprinting.
3. Since DNA from every tissue from an individual show the same degree of polymorphism, they become very useful identification tool in forensic applications.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Satellite DNA is highly repetitive DNA that does not code for any protein, so options $1$ and $2$ are wrong.
2. Satellite DNA shows a high degree of polymorphism between individuals of a population, so option $4$ ('same in all members') is wrong.
3. These polymorphisms are inherited from parents to children, which makes satellite DNA the basis of DNA fingerprinting, so the answer is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** DNA Fingerprinting · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 34)

  The opening clause 'does not code for proteins' is correct, and a student who verifies that clause accepts the second clause riding behind it without testing whether satellite DNA is the same in everyone

## Q129

Biology · botany · Transport in Plants

A column of water within xylem vessels of tall trees does not break under its weight because of :

- (1) Positive root pressure
- (2) Dissolved sugars in water

## Q134

> ⚠ The independent solve chose (2); the booklet prints (4).

- (3) Conidia | Penicillium

4. (3) Conidia | Penicillium (Ascomycetes)
5. (4) Offset | Water hyacinth

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Banana propagates vegetatively by rhizome, Penicillium forms asexual conidia, and water hyacinth spreads by offsets, so options $1$, $3$ and $4$ are correctly matched; Sargassum is a multicellular brown alga that does not undergo binary fission (a unicellular mode), so option $2$ is the incorrect pair.
